Yes. **I think you've identified the two most important architectural issues**, and I would actually change our priority because of them.

## 1. Positions should NOT be a normal “positions” screen

I agree completely.

What you're describing is much more valuable:

> **Positions = Monitor + Diagnose + Adjust**

The screen shouldn't merely say:

| Position | Qty | LTP | P&L |
|---|---:|---:|---:|

>
> **Why are we showing this?**
> - Current NIFTY: X
> - Distance to short call: X points
> - Put-side profit: X%
> - Call-side pressure: X
> - Time to expiry: X
>

> → Prepare orders  
> → User executes

That's a **much stronger product concept** than another Zerodha-style positions table.

---

# 2. I searched specifically around adjustments

There isn't one universally accepted list called “all adjustments.” Different traders use different rulebooks.

But the recurring families include:

### A. Profit-side / untested-side adjustment

Exactly the example you gave.

When one side of a neutral strategy has become profitable while the underlying approaches the other side:

> Take profit on the profitable/untested side and potentially redeploy it closer to the market.

This is commonly described as **rolling the untested side** or **recentering**. Recent Indian educational material on iron-condor adjustment explicitly describes rolling the untested spread closer to collect credit and recentre the payoff. citeturn0search5turn0search8

### B. Roll the threatened short strike

Move the threatened short option further away from the underlying, typically to another strike/expiry.

### C. Roll the entire spread

Move both legs of a vertical spread.

### D. Roll the threatened side

For an Iron Condor:

- call spread → roll
- put spread → roll

depending on which side is threatened.

### E. Roll the untested side

Move the profitable side toward the underlying to collect additional premium / recenter.

### F. Convert structure

Examples:

- Iron Condor → Iron Butterfly
- Spread → wider/narrower spread
- Neutral structure → directional structure

### G. Add a hedge

Potentially add:

- long option
- farther OTM option
- futures hedge

### H. Close profitable side

Simply realize the profitable side rather than continuing to manage it.

### I. Partial close / reduce exposure

Close part of a position rather than the whole structure.

### J. Exit the entire strategy

An important “adjustment” is actually:

> **Don't adjust. Exit.**

This is critical because adjustments can **increase risk**, not necessarily reduce it. Recent material on iron-condor adjustments explicitly warns that adjustments can add capital, widen loss zones or otherwise increase total risk. citeturn0search5

So I would build an **Adjustment Engine**, not a collection of hard-coded “tips.”

The engine evaluates:

**Strategy + Current Market + Time + P&L + Greeks + Position geometry + Predefined rules**

and produces:

> **Possible actions**

—not:

> **You should do X.**

That distinction will also become extremely important when we investigate the **SEBI investment-advice boundary**.

```

And **Zerodha should not be our primary public market-data source.**

# 5. What about Zerodha's data?

Interestingly, **Zerodha's Kite Connect WebSocket is technically capable of providing a lot of what we need**.

It provides streaming quotes, including LTP, OHLC, volume, OI and market depth; current documentation says up to 3,000 instruments per WebSocket connection, with up to three WebSocket connections per API key. citeturn1search0

Kite also provides an instrument master containing:

- expiry
- strike
- tick size
- lot size
- instrument type
- exchange
- instrument token. citeturn1search1

So we absolutely should use Zerodha for:

> **Account + Orders + Trades + Positions + Broker state**

But I **would not make Zerodha our sole market-data dependency**.

Why?

Because our product's market-data requirement is broader than one user's broker connection.

We need a centralized data service that can power:

> Option Chain → Strategy calculations → Monitoring → Alerts → Adjustment engine

independently of whether a particular user happens to be connected to Zerodha at that moment.

---

# 6. There is an even more important issue: licensing

This is where I think your previous project may have hit the real problem.

Getting a WebSocket that technically gives you NIFTY ticks is **not the same thing as being allowed to build a commercial SaaS around that data**.

NSE's current data policy explicitly covers use, sharing, distribution, dissemination and display of market data. Commercial subscribers require an agreement, and redistribution is subject to the agreement's terms. citeturn0search2

TrueData itself also says its data is licensed rather than owned and that redistribution/resale/public sharing requires appropriate permission. citeturn0search3

Therefore:

> **Our first vendor conversation must not be “Can I get an API?”**

It must be:

> **“I am building a commercial multi-user SaaS web platform. Can I legally display your licensed NSE/BSE real-time derivatives data to my authenticated end users, and what commercial redistribution/end-user licensing model applies?”**

That is a **much more important question**.

Yes. **I strongly agree with this direction**, and I think it makes the product substantially better.

I'm locking this as a **core product principle**:

> **The platform does not decide what the “correct” adjustment is. It provides a configurable Adjustment Rules Engine that suggests actions according to the user's own predefined rules.**

That is much more powerful than hard-coding a few adjustment strategies.

## How I would structure it

### 1. Default adjustment playbooks

When a user creates a strategy, the platform can offer **pre-built adjustment rule sets** based on commonly used practices.

For example, for an Iron Condor:

**Situation**
- NIFTY moves toward short Call
- Put side has achieved ≥ 70% of potential profit
- Distance to short Call ≤ X points
- Days to expiry ≤ X

**Possible action**
> Consider taking profit on the Put side and repositioning the Put spread closer to the current market.

But this is only a **default rule**.

---

### 2. User can completely configure the rules

The user should be able to change:

**WHEN**

- Underlying moves X points
- Underlying reaches a specific level
- Distance from short strike
- Distance from breakeven
- Side reaches X% profit
- Strategy reaches X% of max profit
- Strategy reaches X% of max loss
- Delta reaches X
- Gamma reaches X
- Theta reaches X
- IV changes by X%
- IV percentile reaches X
- Time to expiry reaches X days/hours
- Premium reaches X
- P&L changes by X
- Any combination of these

**AND / OR**

conditions.

Then:

**DO**

- Take profit on a leg
- Close a leg
- Roll a leg
- Roll a spread
- Move strike closer
- Move strike farther
- Add a hedge
- Remove a hedge
- Add another leg
- Reduce quantity
- Increase quantity
- Re-center strategy
- Convert strategy
- Exit entire strategy
- Simply alert — **no trade proposal**

And critically:

> **The platform should generate the proposed new strategy and show the user exactly what would change.**

---

> "Iron Condor Adjustment Screen"

Strategy
+
Current Market State
+
Position State
+
Time
+
P&L
+
Greeks
+
User Rules
        ↓
Adjustment Trigger
        ↓
Candidate Adjustment
        ↓
Before / After Analysis
        ↓
User Decision
```

That same engine can eventually support:

### Entry rules
### Adjustment rules
### Exit rules
### Future automated execution

This is architecturally excellent because we're not building four separate rule systems.

---

# 4. I would also give users three levels of control

### Guided

> **“When NIFTY comes within 100 points of my short strike, alert me.”**

### Standard

> If:
> - short strike delta > 0.30
> - AND profitable side > 60%
> - AND DTE < 5
>
> Then:
> **Suggest rolling untested side closer to spot.**

### Advanced

A full rule builder:

> `(Condition A AND Condition B) OR Condition C`

with parameters, comparisons and actions.

---

# 5. Most importantly: suggestions, not recommendations

I would deliberately use language such as:

> **Adjustment opportunity detected**

> **Your rule says this situation qualifies for an adjustment.**

> **Possible adjustment**

> **Review adjustment**

rather than:

> **You should do this.**

That preserves the user's control and is also something we'll need to examine carefully from a **SEBI/compliance** perspective before implementation.

|---|---|

| Sell 23,000 PE | ₹XX |

>

**Close**
- Buy 22,800 PE
- Sell 23,000 PE

- Sell 23,150 PE

|---|---:|---:|

| Max Loss | ₹X | ₹Y |

And a new payoff graph.

The user decides.

# 7. We should support multiple adjustment rules

A user could have:

**Rule 1 — Profit-based**
> If one side reaches 70% profit → suggest taking profit.

**Rule 2 — Strike proximity**
> If NIFTY comes within 100 points of short strike → suggest adjustment.

**Rule 3 — Delta**
> If short option Delta exceeds 0.30 → alert.

**Rule 4 — Loss**
> If strategy loses ₹5,000 → suggest exit.

**Rule 5 — Expiry**
> If DTE ≤ 2 → suggest closing strategy.

The system evaluates all of them.

If multiple rules trigger simultaneously, the platform should show:

> **3 adjustment conditions triggered**

and intelligently group them rather than bombarding the user with three separate notifications.

### **“Why am I seeing this?”**

Every adjustment suggestion should explain **exactly which user rule triggered it**.

For example:

>
> Your rule:
> “When the tested short strike reaches Delta 0.30 and the opposite side has achieved 70% profit.”
>
> Current conditions:
> - Short Call Delta: **0.34**
> - Put-side profit: **74%**
>
> Both conditions are met.

That creates enormous trust.

Yes — and this research changes my recommendation slightly.

First, **Question 57 is locked: C — Global defaults + per-strategy overrides** for adjustment rules. 

Now to your important data question:

## Short answer

**Both TrueData and GDFL are technically capable of supporting our platform, but their ordinary API subscriptions are NOT sufficient for the SaaS use case you described.**

For your requirement:

> “Commercial multi-user SaaS web platform where authenticated end users see licensed NSE/BSE real-time derivatives data”

we need a **commercial redistribution/display licensing arrangement**, not a normal individual API subscription.

That is actually good news because **both vendors explicitly acknowledge this distinction.**

---

# 1. TrueData

TrueData is technically an excellent fit.

Their current API offering supports:

- NSE
- NFO
- BSE
- real-time streaming
- WebSocket
- REST
- option chain
- bid/ask
- OI
- volume
- IV
- Delta
- Gamma
- Theta
- Vega
- historical data

They specifically advertise their APIs for fintech applications and financial platforms. citeturn0search0

However, their published terms are very important.

Their standard API terms say the data is for the **subscriber's own use** and cannot simply be shared, transmitted, redistributed or displayed to third parties. They explicitly state that **redistribution, commercial display, enterprise-wide access or third-party use requires appropriate approval/licensing from the exchanges**. citeturn0search0turn0search1

So:

### Normal TrueData API
❌ Not sufficient for our SaaS.

### TrueData + appropriate commercial exchange licensing
✅ Potentially suitable.

---

# 2. Global Datafeeds (GDFL)

GDFL is also technically very suitable.

Their current API offering supports:

- NSE/NFO
- BSE/BFO
- WebSocket
- REST
- real-time
- historical
- option chain
- option Greeks
- entire exchange data

They specifically describe their WebSocket API as suitable for **web, mobile and desktop applications**. citeturn0search4

But their licensing language is even more explicit.

GDFL currently says:

> API access is for individual users for personal use.

For **commercial use**, they say the customer needs to sign an agreement with the relevant exchanges and pay the applicable fees. citeturn0search6

Their general terms also say realtime/IEOD data is for the subscriber and may not be redistributed without explicit written permission. citeturn0search3

So:

### Normal GDFL API
❌ Not sufficient for our SaaS.

### GDFL + commercial exchange licensing/permission
✅ Potentially suitable.

---

# 3. And NSE's own policy confirms this

This is the most important source.

NSE's current **Data Sharing & Usage Policy** explicitly covers:

- use
- sharing
- distribution
- redistribution
- dissemination
- display

of market data, including derivatives data. citeturn0search2

For commercial use, NSE says the subscriber must enter into the relevant agreement, and the agreement specifies how the data may be:

> received, transmitted, handled, disseminated or otherwise dealt with.

The agreement can also contain **end-user restrictions**. citeturn0search2

And importantly:

> Redistribution is only permitted as agreed in the relevant agreement.

citeturn0search2

So our proposed architecture needs to be designed around the **actual license**, not around whatever an API happens to technically allow.

---

# 4. Therefore, I would change our data-provider decision

Earlier I suggested:

> TrueData vs GDFL

I still recommend evaluating both, but **the first question to vendors should now be commercial licensing**, not API quality.

I would send both vendors essentially this exact requirement:

> **We are building a commercial, multi-user SaaS web platform for Indian derivatives traders. The platform will have authenticated users who can view licensed real-time NSE/NFO and BSE/BFO market data, including option-chain data, LTP, bid/ask, volume, OI and potentially Greeks. The data will be displayed inside our web application and used by our proprietary strategy/risk/analytics engine.**
>
> **We need to understand the commercial licensing/redistribution model required for this use case, including exchange licensing, per-user fees, display rights, derived-data rights, caching/storage rights, historical-data rights, API/server architecture restrictions, and permitted number of end users.**
>
> **Can you provide the appropriate commercial/API product and licensing terms for this SaaS use case?**

That will get us a much more useful answer than asking:

> “Do you have an API?”

---

# 5. There is another critical distinction

Our application will not merely **display** data.

We'll also **derive information from it**.

For example:

```text
Market Data
     ↓
Our Calculation Engine
     ↓
Strategy P&L
     ↓
Greeks
     ↓
Risk
     ↓
Adjustment Conditions
     ↓
Adjustment Suggestions
```

So we need to ask the vendor/exchange specifically about **derived data**.

For example:

### Raw data
NIFTY = 23,180

### Derived data
Distance to short strike = 120 points

### More derived
Short strike Delta = 0.31

### Product-derived
> “Your configured adjustment condition has been triggered.”

We need to know what we are permitted to **display to users**, what can be **stored**, and what can be **redistributed**, even when the displayed number isn't literally the exchange's raw tick.

NSE's policy says the relevant agreement governs how market data may be used, transmitted and disseminated, so I would make this an explicit part of the vendor negotiation. citeturn0search2

| Greeks | ✅ | ✅ |

| Commercial SaaS | **Requires appropriate licensing** | **Requires appropriate licensing** |
| Public/client display | **Special approval/license** | **Special approval/license** |
| Good technical candidate | ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ | ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ |

I **would not choose between them yet**.

Instead, I'd approach both with the exact SaaS requirement and compare:

1. Commercial licensing
2. Exchange fees
3. Per-user fees
4. Minimum commitment
5. Number of users allowed
6. Web display rights
7. Raw-data redistribution restrictions
8. Derived-data rights
9. Historical storage rights
10. Caching rights
11. WebSocket connection limits
12. API rate limits
13. Option-chain API limits
14. SLA
15. Support
16. Sandbox/test environment
17. Whether we can maintain **one centralized vendor connection** and distribute permitted data to authenticated users
18. Whether their agreement covers **NSE + BSE derivatives simultaneously**

That last part is particularly important because our V1 includes **NIFTY and SENSEX**.

---

# 8. One architectural consequence

I still recommend the centralized architecture I described earlier:

**Vendor → Our Market Data Gateway → Our internal normalized feed → Users**

rather than:

**Every browser → Vendor**

But **whether we are legally allowed to redistribute that feed internally to authenticated SaaS users is something the commercial agreement must explicitly permit.**

So the architecture should be **license-aware** from day one.

**A. Verify through your Zerodha Authorized Partner/referral records — Recommended**

Your system receives/imports the relevant customer/referral attribution information and matches it to the user's platform account.

**B. User enters their Zerodha client ID and we manually verify it**

**C. User uploads/provides proof and Admin manually approves**

**D. Combination: automated verification first, Admin review for exceptions**

I recommend **D** for production: automated verification wherever possible, with an Admin review workflow for cases that cannot be automatically matched.

This is particularly important because **permanent free Pro is a valuable entitlement**, so we should make the attribution auditable and resistant to abuse.

=== #236 ASSISTANT 2026-09-15T16:24 ===
Yes, I understand exactly what you're protecting against. This is **trial/identity abuse**, and I strongly recommend we design for it now rather than trying to patch it later.

### My recommendation

Treat the **Zerodha Client ID as a unique commercial identity**, separate from Gmail.

For example:

**Platform Account A**
- Gmail: `abc@gmail.com`
- Zerodha Client ID: `Z12345`
- 7-day trial: Used

Later:

**Platform Account B**
- Gmail: `xyz@gmail.com`
- Attempts to connect: `Z12345`

The system should detect:

> ⚠️ **This Zerodha account has already been associated with another platform account.**  
> Your 7-day Pro trial is not available again for this Zerodha account.

And importantly, **disconnecting Zerodha from Account A must not erase this history.**

### I would make the underlying rule slightly stronger

We should maintain an **Identity & Entitlement Abuse Prevention layer**, rather than simply a `trial_used` flag.

It should track relationships such as:

`Google Account ↔ Platform User ↔ Zerodha Client ID ↔ Trial History ↔ Pro Entitlements ↔ Referral History`

This also helps prevent future abuse involving:
- multiple Gmail accounts
- repeated 7-day trials
- referral manipulation
- repeatedly disconnecting/reconnecting the same Zerodha account
- attempting to obtain referral-earned benefits multiple times

But there is an important exception: **legitimate account recovery/change of email.** We shouldn't permanently trap a genuine user just because they need to change their Gmail account. That should require an explicit recovery/verification process rather than automatically creating a second trial.

---

## One important question

### Q82 — What should happen when the **same Zerodha Client ID is connected to a second Gmail account**?

**A. Strict block — my recommendation**  
Reject the Zerodha connection to the second account completely. Tell them the Zerodha account is already associated with another platform account. They can contact support if this is genuinely their account and they need an account/email change.

**B. Allow connection, but no new trial**  
Allow the second Gmail account to connect to that Zerodha account, but immediately put it into Limited/Read-Only mode because the 7-day trial has already been consumed.

**C. Allow only after verification**  
Initially block it, but provide a verification/recovery process. If successfully verified, transfer the Zerodha association from the old platform account to the new one, while **never resetting the trial history**.

**D. Your own approach**

### My recommendation: **C**

It gives us strong anti-abuse protection without creating a terrible experience for a genuine customer who legitimately needs to change their Gmail/platform account.

**Which do you choose: A, B, or C?**

=== #238 ASSISTANT 2026-09-15T16:27 ===
Exactly. This is an important distinction. **Direct Qualifying Zerodha Customers should have a different recovery/identity policy from ordinary trial users.**

I would design it like this:

### Direct Zerodha customer scenario

Suppose the qualifying Zerodha Client ID is `Z12345`.

Initially:

**Account A**
- Gmail: `wrong@gmail.com`
- Zerodha: `Z12345`
- Authentication succeeds
- System verifies `Z12345` is on our qualifying-customer list
- → **Pro, ₹0, indefinitely**

Later the customer realizes they should have used:

**Account B**
- Gmail: `correct@gmail.com`
- Wants to connect `Z12345`

We should **not say:**  
> "This Zerodha account is already used. Access denied."

That would punish a legitimate customer.

Instead:

> **This Zerodha account is already linked to another account.**  
> We found an existing platform account associated with this Zerodha Client ID.  
>
> If you created that account by mistake and want to use this Gmail account instead, you can transfer your account association after verification.
>
> **Verify & Switch Account**

### What happens after verification?

I recommend **transferring the Zerodha association**, rather than creating two independent Pro accounts.

So:

`wrong@gmail.com → Z12345`

becomes:

`correct@gmail.com → Z12345`

And the old account becomes something like:

> **Account association transferred**  
> Please sign in using your new account.

Most importantly:

**The Pro entitlement does NOT get consumed, reset, or converted into a trial.**

Because the entitlement belongs to the qualifying **Zerodha Client ID**, not to the Gmail address.

---

### This gives us two different rules

| Situation | Same Zerodha Client ID used elsewhere? | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Ordinary user trying to get another 7-day trial | Yes | ❌ No second trial |
| Ordinary user legitimately changing account | Yes | ✅ Recovery/transfer after verification |
| Direct qualifying Zerodha customer | Yes | ✅ Recovery/transfer after verification |
| Direct qualifying customer moves to new Gmail | Yes | ✅ Full complimentary Pro continues |
| User disconnects Zerodha and reconnects with another Gmail | Yes | ❌ Cannot reset trial/benefits |

### One more safeguard I strongly recommend

The **transfer itself should be auditable and rate-limited**.

For example:

- old platform account
- new platform account
- Zerodha Client ID
- timestamp
- reason
- verification method
- admin/system approval
- previous entitlement history
- new entitlement history

And we should avoid allowing someone to repeatedly bounce:

`Gmail A → Gmail B → Gmail C → Gmail A`

without controls.

So the underlying principle becomes:

> **Gmail identifies the platform account. Zerodha Client ID identifies the broker identity. Entitlements belong to the appropriate underlying identity/event, not merely to an email address.**

I think this is the right architecture because it handles both **fraud prevention and genuine customer mistakes**.

**C. Admin/support approval:** user raises a request and your admin manually approves the transfer.

**D. Hybrid:** automatic transfer for straightforward cases; suspicious/repeated transfers go to Admin review.

I recommend **D**.

### 1. Zerodha session ≠ Zerodha identity

If the Zerodha authorization/session expires every ~24 hours, that should simply mean:

**Connection status**
`Connected → Session Expired`

The user then authenticates with Zerodha again.

It should **not** mean:

`Zerodha Client ID → disconnected identity`

We should retain the verified Zerodha Client ID association permanently.

So every morning:

> **Zerodha connection expired**  
> Reconnect to continue using broker-dependent features.

After successful Zerodha authentication:

> ✅ **Zerodha reconnected**  
> Client ID verified: `Z12345`

No new trial. No new account. No new entitlement.

The system should recognize:

**Same Platform User + Same Zerodha Client ID**

even though the **Zerodha API session/token changes every day**.

Conceptually:

```text
Platform User
     │
     └── Zerodha Identity: Z12345  ← persistent
             │
             ├── Session: 14 Sep → expired
             ├── Session: 15 Sep → expired
             ├── Session: 16 Sep → expired
             └── ...
```

The session is temporary.  
The broker identity is persistent.

### And for the fraud scenario

If:

`Gmail A → Z12345`

has already consumed its trial, then:

`Gmail B → Z12345`

cannot get another trial simply because the old Zerodha session expired.

The system checks the **persistent Zerodha Client ID**, not the current Zerodha session.

That is exactly the distinction we should put into the architecture.

---

Your clarification means we should **not treat the daily Zerodha disconnection as an account-disconnection event at all**.

We should have three separate concepts:

1. **Zerodha Identity** — persistent Client ID
2. **Zerodha Authorization Session** — temporary, expires/requires re-authentication
3. **Platform Entitlement** — trial/complimentary/referral/paid status

They must never be conflated.

I would therefore **keep Q96 open** rather than locking it based on the assumption that users intentionally disconnect their Zerodha account.

**B. No — deletion completely resets the relationship.**

**C. Retain it for a configurable period, then purge it.**

Absolutely. I’ll treat this as the **official V1 foundation** for identity, trial, entitlement and anti-abuse, and use it as the baseline for all later UX/backend decisions.

:::writing{variant="document" id="58321" title="Identity, Trial, Entitlement & Anti-Abuse Specification — V1"}
# Identity, Trial, Entitlement & Anti-Abuse Specification — V1

## 1. Core Principle

The platform must distinguish between:

1. **Platform identity** — who the user is on our platform.
2. **Google identity** — how the user signs in.
3. **Mobile identity** — verified through WhatsApp OTP.
4. **Zerodha identity** — the user's persistent Zerodha Client ID.
5. **Zerodha authorization session** — temporary authorization/session used to access Zerodha services.
6. **Commercial entitlement** — why the user currently has Pro access.

These identities must never be treated as interchangeable.

---

## 2. Platform Account

Every registered user receives a permanent internal:

`Platform User ID`

Changing a Gmail address does not create a new Platform User ID.

The Platform User ID owns:

- Strategies
- Historical simulations
- Strategy history
- Execution history
- Adjustment history
- Notifications/preferences
- Referral history
- Subscription history
- Entitlement history
- Zerodha association

---

## 3. Google Authentication

Google Sign-In is the preferred authentication mechanism.

The Google-authenticated email is treated as verified.

A user may change their email through an authenticated account-change process.

When an email is changed:

- Platform User ID remains unchanged.
- Zerodha association remains unchanged.
- Trial history remains unchanged.
- Pro entitlement remains unchanged.
- Strategies/history remain unchanged.
- Old email immediately stops being a login identifier.
- Old email remains only in security/audit history.

Changing email can never be used to obtain another trial.

---

## 4. Mobile Verification

Mobile number is captured during registration and verified through WhatsApp OTP.

Store:

- Country code
- Mobile number
- Verification status
- Verification timestamp
- Relevant OTP security/rate-limit metadata

For V1, mobile identity is **not** used as the primary trial-uniqueness mechanism.

---

## 5. Zerodha Identity

The **Zerodha Client ID is the persistent broker identity**.

A successful Zerodha authentication establishes that the authenticated Zerodha account belongs to that Client ID.

The platform must retain the relationship:

`Zerodha Client ID → Platform User ID`

even when the Zerodha authorization session expires.

---

## 6. Zerodha Authorization Session

The temporary Zerodha authorization/session is separate from the Zerodha identity.

When the Zerodha session expires, the platform should show:

> Zerodha connection expired  
> Re-authenticate with Zerodha to continue broker-dependent functionality.

The user authenticates again.

A new authorization session must:

- Reuse the existing Zerodha Client ID
- Restore the existing Platform User ↔ Zerodha relationship
- Never create a new trial
- Never create a new customer identity
- Never reset entitlements

Therefore:

**Session expiry ≠ identity deletion.**

---

## 7. One-to-One Zerodha Association

V1 rule:

> **One Zerodha Client ID can be actively associated with only one Platform User ID at a time.**

Example:

`Gmail A → Platform User A → Zerodha Z12345`

A second platform account attempting:

`Gmail B → Zerodha Z12345`

must not independently obtain access/trial.

The platform must detect the existing association.

---

## 8. Legitimate Account Transfer

A legitimate user may accidentally create their account using the wrong Gmail and later want to use another Gmail.

We support an account-transfer/recovery process.

Example:

`old@gmail.com → Z12345`

to:

`new@gmail.com → Z12345`

The normal flow should:

1. Detect existing Zerodha association.
2. Verify ownership/authentication.
3. Verify the new platform identity.
4. Determine whether the transfer is normal or suspicious.
5. Automatically complete legitimate cases.
6. Route suspicious/repeated cases to Admin review.

The transfer must:

- Preserve Platform User data where appropriate.
- Preserve Zerodha association.
- Preserve trial history.
- Preserve Pro entitlement.
- Preserve referral history.
- Never issue a second trial.

---

## 9. Direct Qualifying Zerodha Customers

The Admin maintains a qualifying Zerodha Client ID list.

Eligibility flow:

1. User authenticates with Zerodha.
2. Platform obtains/verifies the Client ID.
3. Platform checks the Admin-maintained eligibility list.
4. Match → Direct Qualifying Zerodha Customer.
5. User receives full Pro access indefinitely at ₹0.

Entitlement representation:

`Plan = Pro`

`Price = ₹0`

`Entitlement Source = Direct Zerodha Customer`

`Expiry = None`

The entitlement belongs to the qualifying Zerodha identity, not merely to a Gmail address.

Therefore, a legitimate email change does not remove the complimentary Pro benefit.

---

## 10. Seven-Day Trial

Every new platform registration receives:

**7 days of full Pro access.**

The trial begins when registration is successfully completed.

Zerodha connection is **not required** to start the trial.

The user can explore the product without connecting Zerodha.

---

## 11. Trial Uniqueness When Zerodha Is Connected

If a Zerodha Client ID has previously consumed a platform trial:

`Z12345 → Trial Used`

then another platform account cannot obtain another trial by connecting the same Client ID.

Example:

`Gmail A → Z12345 → Trial Used`

Later:

`Gmail B → Z12345`

Result:

> This Zerodha account has already been associated with another platform account. Your 7-day Pro trial is not available again for this Zerodha account.

The user may use the legitimate recovery/transfer process if appropriate.

---

## 12. Users Who Never Connect Zerodha

For V1, if a user:

`Gmail A → registers → never connects Zerodha → trial expires`

and later creates another platform account:

`Gmail B → registers → never connects Zerodha`

we do **not** attempt to identify the same human through aggressive fingerprinting.

The second account may receive its own trial.

Basic platform abuse controls remain applicable, but V1 does not attempt to establish a universal “one human = one trial” identity.

This deliberately keeps registration friction low.

---

## 13. Trial Expiry

When the 7-day trial expires:

User enters:

**Limited / Read-Only Mode**

They retain access to:

- Existing strategies
- Historical strategies
- Historical simulations
- Historical P&L
- Reports
- Account/settings
- Free Eligibility
- Referrals
- Billing
- Important monitoring of already-active strategies

They cannot:

- Create new Pro strategies
- Prepare new trades
- Execute new trades
- Run new Pro simulations
- Access live Option Chain functionality

---

## 14. Active Strategies After Expiry

An important safety exception applies.

If the user has an active strategy when Pro expires:

**The platform continues monitoring that strategy.**

The user continues receiving relevant risk/exit/monitoring alerts.

The platform must not create a situation where:

> "Your subscription expired, therefore we stopped showing you the risk of your already-active strategy."

Commercial restrictions and safety monitoring are therefore separated.

---

## 15. Live Option Chain After Expiry

The live/detailed Option Chain is a Pro feature.

A Limited/Read-Only user opening Option Chain sees a Pro gate.

Example:

> **Live Option Chain is a Pro feature**  
> Your Pro access has expired. Upgrade to Pro or check whether you qualify for complimentary access through Zerodha.
>
> **[Subscribe to Pro] [Check Free Eligibility]**

A static/sample preview may be displayed, but must be clearly labelled:

**Sample / Not Live**

No licensed real-time market data should be exposed.

---

## 16. Historical Data

User-generated historical information remains available after Pro expiry.

This includes:

- Previously created strategies
- Completed strategies
- Historical simulations/results
- Historical P&L
- Reports
- Strategy configurations
- Adjustment history
- Execution history

However:

> Existing results can be viewed; new Pro computations/simulations remain gated.

---

## 17. Zerodha Disconnection

A Zerodha connection/session ending does **not** delete or close a strategy.

The Strategy is a platform object.

Zerodha is an external broker/account-data/execution connection.

When Zerodha is unavailable:

Available:

- Strategy
- Historical information
- Strategy configuration
- Historical analysis

Unavailable until reconnection:

- Live Zerodha positions
- Zerodha orders
- Zerodha margin/account information
- Broker-dependent execution
- Other broker-dependent functionality

The user receives:

> **Reconnect Zerodha to continue**

---

## 18. Same Zerodha Client ID Reconnection

If the same Platform User later authenticates with the same Zerodha Client ID:

`Platform User A → Z12345`

the platform restores the existing relationship.

No:

- New trial
- New account
- New entitlement
- New customer record

is created.

---

## 19. Account Deletion

Deleting the platform account must not automatically reset trial eligibility associated with a previously connected Zerodha Client ID.

After account deletion, retain the **minimum necessary anti-abuse/entitlement record**, subject to the final privacy and data-retention policy.

Example minimal record:

- Zerodha Client ID or appropriately protected identifier
- Trial-consumed status
- Relevant entitlement history identifier
- Security/audit reference
- Necessary timestamps

Do not retain the entire deleted user account merely for trial prevention.

---

## 20. Entitlement Engine

Access must not be implemented as:

`free_user = true/false`

Instead, use an entitlement system.

Possible entitlement sources:

- Trial Pro
- Direct Zerodha Customer Pro
- Referral-earned Pro
- Paid Monthly Pro
- Paid Annual Pro
- Expired
- Limited/Read-Only

Each entitlement should have:

- Type
- Source
- Start date/time
- End date/time, if applicable
- Status
- Origin/reference
- Creation reason
- Audit history

---

## 21. Entitlement Priority

Where multiple entitlements exist, the system evaluates them according to defined rules rather than overwriting one with another.

Example:

A user may have:

`Direct Qualifying Zerodha Pro — indefinite`

plus:

`Referral reward — +30 days`

The system should preserve the referral event/history even though the direct entitlement already provides Pro access.

Entitlements are events/history, not a single boolean flag.

---

## 22. Referral Entitlement

Initial rule:

**One successful qualifying Zerodha referral = one month Pro free.**

A successful referral means the referred person:

1. Opens a Zerodha account through the user's qualifying referral path.
2. Is successfully attributed to the user.

A click/application start is not sufficient.

Referral rewards are recorded as entitlement events.

Admin-configurable rules will eventually include:

- Reward duration
- Stacking
- Maximum accumulated free days
- Validity period
- Attribution rules
- Fraud controls

Initial stacking configuration: **enabled**.

---

## 23. Paid Pro

Paid Pro remains available independently of the Zerodha entitlement system.

V1:

- Monthly Pro: ₹600/month
- Annual Pro: Admin-configurable
- Payment provider: Razorpay
- Trial: 7 days
- Commercial parameters configurable by Admin

Direct qualifying Zerodha customers have:

`Pro = ₹0`

and do not need to pay.

---

## 24. Paid Pro Expiry

For V1, when paid Pro expires:

`Active Pro → Limited/Read-Only`

The same safety rule applies:

**Existing active strategies continue to be monitored.**

No new strategy creation/preparation/execution is allowed until Pro access is restored.

---

## 25. Anti-Abuse Controls

V1 should specifically protect against:

### Protected scenario 1
Different Gmail → same Zerodha Client ID → second trial.

**Blocked.**

### Protected scenario 2
Disconnect/reconnect same Zerodha account → second trial.

**Blocked.**

### Protected scenario 3
Delete account → recreate with another Gmail → same Zerodha Client ID → second trial.

**Blocked.**

### Protected scenario 4
Same Zerodha Client ID simultaneously used by two platform accounts.

**Blocked.**

### Protected scenario 5
Change Gmail on the same platform account → trial reset.

**Blocked.**

### Allowed scenario
Different legitimate Zerodha Client ID.

**Allowed to have its own trial history.**

---

## 26. Suspicious Account Transfers

Automatic transfer should be possible for ordinary legitimate cases.

Potential triggers for Admin review include:

- Repeated transfers
- Multiple recent email changes
- Repeated association attempts
- Unusual account-creation patterns
- Conflicting identity information
- Other configurable fraud signals

The system should not automatically accuse the user of fraud.

Use neutral language:

> **We need to verify this account change.**

---

## 27. Audit Trail

Identity and entitlement events should be auditable.

Examples:

- Platform account created
- Google identity added/changed
- Mobile verified
- Zerodha Client ID associated
- Zerodha session authenticated
- Zerodha session expired
- Trial started
- Trial expired
- Direct customer eligibility granted/revoked
- Referral reward granted
- Subscription started
- Subscription expired
- Email changed
- Zerodha association transferred
- Transfer sent to Admin review
- Account deleted
- Anti-abuse restriction triggered

---

## 28. User-Facing Principle

Never expose internal fraud terminology unnecessarily.

Do not say:

> "You are suspected of trial abuse."

Prefer:

> "This Zerodha account has already been associated with another platform account."

For a legitimate recovery case:

> "If you previously created an account using a different email, you can verify your identity and move your account association."

---

## 29. Architectural Principle

The system should be designed around:

**Identity → Entitlement → Capability**

rather than:

**Login → Free/Paid flag**

This allows future commercial models without redesigning the core product.

---

## 30. Final V1 Identity Rule

The most important rule is:

> **A Gmail address can change. A Zerodha session can expire. A platform account can be recovered. But the historical relationship between a Zerodha Client ID and platform entitlements must not be reset merely by changing credentials, sessions, or email addresses.**
:::

With this foundation locked, I recommend we now return to the **screen architecture**, starting with the pending **Q81 — Home for a brand-new user**. That keeps us aligned with your earlier goal of establishing the overall product structure before going deep into individual features.

### Q81 — Home for a brand-new user

My recommendation remains an **action-oriented empty Home**, not a trading terminal:

- **Welcome, [Name]**
- **Plan your trade. Follow your strategy. Then execute.**
- **Create a Strategy**
- **Explore Option Chain**
- **Learn**
- **7-day Pro Trial — Active**
- A small **“I already know my strategy”** shortcut for experienced users
- No forced Zerodha connection

The Zerodha connection should appear **only when the user actually requests a broker-dependent function**.

**Do you approve this Home structure?**

Yes. We’ll continue from **Q33A** and keep all the decisions already made as locked. We’ll also continue **one question at a time with my recommendation**.

### Q33A — What should the scenario P&L represent?

This is important because the strategy table needs to distinguish between a **deterministic expiry payoff** and what the position might be worth **right now**.

**A. Expiry P&L only**  
For each scenario price, calculate what the strategy makes/loses **if the position is held until expiry**.

**B. Estimated current P&L only**  
Estimate what the strategy would be worth at each scenario price **right now**, considering remaining time, volatility, option pricing, etc.

**C. Both — my recommendation** ⭐  
Show both, but make **Expiry P&L the primary scenario view**.

Why I recommend **C**:

- Expiry P&L is transparent and deterministic.
- It makes the payoff shape easy for beginners to understand.
- Current estimated P&L is much more useful for a live strategy that still has days/weeks to expiry.
- It prevents users from confusing a theoretical expiry payoff with a model-based estimate of today's position value.

For example, at NIFTY 23,000:

> **Expiry scenario P&L:** +₹6,825  
> **Estimated current P&L at 23,000:** calculated using current time-to-expiry, IV, pricing model, etc.

This also gives us a much stronger foundation for the later **Adjustment Engine**, because adjustments need current-position economics rather than expiry payoff alone.

**My recommendation: C — both, with Expiry P&L as the default primary scenario view.**

What do you choose: **A, B, or C?**

## IMPORTANT PRODUCT PRINCIPLES

Preserve these principles from the previous discussions:

### Strategy-first

The product is not intended to be an unrestricted trading terminal.

The user creates/follows a strategy.

The platform helps analyse, monitor, adjust and execute that strategy.

### User remains in control

The platform should inform and structure decisions rather than make discretionary investment decisions for the user.

Use language such as:
- “Strategies you could consider”
- “Suggested setup”
- “Adjustment opportunity detected”
- “Possible adjustment”
- “Review adjustment”
- “Your rule was triggered”

Avoid language such as:
- “You should take this trade”
- “This is the best trade”
- Guaranteed profit/outcome claims

### Zerodha

Zerodha is the V1 broker.

The user's Zerodha credentials/PIN/OTP must never be requested by the platform.

Zerodha authentication is separate from platform login.

A Zerodha Client ID is a persistent identity relationship, while a Zerodha authorization/session is temporary.

Do not confuse session expiry with identity deletion.

### Commercial model

The product uses an entitlement model rather than a simple free/paid boolean.

Important established commercial states include:
- 7-day Pro trial
- Direct qualifying Zerodha customer → Pro free indefinitely
- Referral-earned Pro
- Paid Monthly Pro
- Paid Annual Pro
- Limited/Read-Only after Pro expiry

### Data architecture

Do not design the browser as a direct connection to the market-data vendor.

The architecture uses a market-data gateway/normalization/cache/event architecture.

Market-data licensing for commercial multi-user SaaS must be considered.

### Risk / adjustment

The platform can identify opportunities based on the user's predefined rules.

It should not present one universally “correct” adjustment.

Adjustments can increase risk, so do not describe them automatically as risk-reducing.

### Execution

Zerodha is the final authority for broker validity, margin and execution.

Execution must account for:
- Margin
- Multi-leg dependencies
- Partial failures
- Failed orders
- Retry
- Strategy completion
- Position reconciliation

Do not automatically unwind successful legs unless that behavior has been explicitly decided.

## IF SOMETHING IS NOT IN THE DOCUMENTS

If a piece of information is genuinely missing:

- Do not invent it.
- Do not assume it was decided.
- Ask me a single clarification question if it is necessary.
- If it is not yet necessary, leave it open.

Think like a pragmatic principal product architect designing for:

- Solo-founder maintainability
- Build velocity
- Reliability
- Performance
- Security
- Scalability to a multi-tenant SaaS
- Indian market/broker realities
- Good UX for beginners and advanced users
- Compliance considerations

Do not choose technology merely because it is trendy.

Explain trade-offs briefly and clearly.

- Whether it is locked

- Remaining dependencies
